          }, Rest}.\n   766→\n   767→\n   768→%% Description: Converts from the ssh name of elliptic curves to\n   769→%% the OIDs.\n   770→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   771→ssh_curvename2oid(<<\"ssh-ed25519\">>) -> ?'id-Ed25519';\n   772→ssh_curvename2oid(<<\"ssh-ed448\">>  ) -> 775→ssh_curvename2oid(<<\"ecdsa-sha2-nistp521\">>) -> ?'secp521r1'.\n   776→\n   777→%% Description: Converts from elliptic curve OIDs to the ssh name.\n   778→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   779→oid2ssh_curvename(?'id-Ed25519')-> {<<\"ssh-ed25519\">>, 'n/a'};\n   780→oid2ssh_curvename(?'id-Ed448')  -> 794→bin_foldl(Fun, Acc0, Bin0) ->\n   795→    case Fun(Bin0,Acc0) of\n   796→        {Bin0,Acc0} ->\n   797→            Acc0;\n   798→        {Bin,Acc} ->\n   799→            bin_foldl(Fun, Acc, The default\n   228→`t:algs_list/0` can be obtained from `default_algorithms/0`.\n   229→\n   230→If an alg_entry() is missing in the algs_list(), the default value is used for\n   231→that entry.\n   232→\n   233→Here is an example of this option:\n   234→\n   235→```erlang\n   236→\t  {preferred_algorithms,\n   237→\t  [{public_key,['ssh-rsa','ssh-dss']},\n   238→\t  {cipher,[{client2server,['aes128-ctr']},\n   239→          {server2client,['aes128-cbc','3des-cbc']}]},\n   240→\t  {mac,['hmac-sha2-256','hmac-sha1']},\n   241→\t  {compression,[none,zlib]}\n   242→\t  ]\n   243→\t  }\n   244→```\n   245→\n   246→The example specifies different algorithms in the two directions (client2server\n   247→and server2client), for cipher but specifies the same algorithms for mac and\n   248→compression in both directions. The kex (key exchange) is implicit but\n   249→public_key is set explicitly.\n   250→\n   251→For background and more examples see the\n   252→[User's Guide](configure_algos.md#introduction).\n   253→\n   254→If an algorithm name occurs more than once in a list, the behaviour is\n   255→undefined. The tags in the property lists are also assumed to occur at most one\n   256→time.\n   257→\n   258→> #### Warning {: .warning }\n   259→>\n   260→> Changing the values can make a connection less secure. Do not change unless\n   261→> you know exactly what you are doing. If you do not understand the values then\n   262→> you are not supposed to change them.\n   263→\"\"\".\n   264→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   265→-type double_algs(AlgType)  :: list( {client2server,[AlgType]} | {server2client,[AlgType]} )\n   266→                             | [AlgType].\n   267→\n   268→-doc \"\"\"\n   269→Modifies the list of algorithms to use in the algorithm negotiation. The\n   270→modifications are applied after the option `preferred_algorithms` (if existing)\n   271→is applied.\n   272→\n   273→The algorithm for modifications works like this:\n   274→\n   275→- Input is the `t:modify_algs_list/0` and a set of algorithms `A` obtained from\n   276→  the `preferred_algorithms` option if existing, or else from the\n   277→  [ssh:default_algorithms/0](`default_algorithms/0`).\n   278→- The head of the `t:modify_algs_list/0` modifies `A` giving the result `A'`.\n   279→\n   280→  The possible modifications are:\n   281→\n   282→  - Append or prepend supported but not enabled algorithm(s) to the list of\n   283→    algorithms. If the wanted algorithms already are in `A` they will first be\n   284→    removed and then appended or prepended,\n   285→  - Remove (rm) one or more algorithms from `A`.\n   286→\n   287→- Repeat the modification step with the tail of `t:modify_algs_list/0` and the\n   288→  resulting `A'`.\n   289→\n   290→If an unsupported algorithm is in the `t:modify_algs_list/0`, it will be\n   291→silently ignored\n   292→\n   293→If there are more than one modify_algorithms options, the result is undefined.\n   294→\n   295→Here is an example of this option:\n   296→\n   297→```text\n   298→\t  {modify_algorithms,\n   299→\t  [{prepend, [{kex, ['diffie-hellman-group1-sha1']}],\n   300→\t  {rm,      [{compression, [none]}]}\n   301→\t  ]\n   302→\t  }\n   303→```\n   304→\n   305→The example specifies that:\n   306→\n   307→- the old key exchange algorithm 'diffie-hellman-group1-sha1' should be the main\n   308→  alternative. It will be the main alternative since it is prepened to the list\n   309→- The compression algorithm none (= no compression) is removed so compression is\n   310→  enforced\n   311→\n   312→For background and more examples see the\n   313→[User's Guide](configure_algos.md#introduction).\n   314→\"\"\".\n   315→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   316→-type modify_algs_list()      :: list( {append,algs_list()} | {prepend,algs_list()} | {rm,algs_list()} ) .\n   317→\n   318→-type internal_options()      :: ssh_options:private_options().\n   319→-type socket_options()        :: [gen_tcp:connect_option() | gen_tcp:listen_option()].\n   320→\n   321→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   322→-type client_options()        :: [ client_option() ] .\n   323→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   324→-type daemon_options()        :: [ daemon_option() ].\n   325→\n   326→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   327→-type common_options() :: [ common_option() ].\n   328→-doc \"\"\"\n   329→The options above can be used both in clients and in daemons (servers). Both the max time and max\n   391→amount of data could be configured:\n   392→\n   393→- `{Minutes, Bytes}` initiate rekeying when any of the limits are reached.\n   394→- `Bytes` initiate rekeying when `Bytes` number of bytes are transferred, or at\n   395→  latest after one hour.\n   396→\n   397→When a rekeying is done, both the timer and the byte counter are restarted.\n   398→Defaults to one hour and one GByte.\n   399→\n   400→If `Minutes` is set to `infinity`, no rekeying will ever occur due to that max\n   401→time has passed. Setting `Bytes` to `infinity` will inhibit rekeying after a\n   402→certain amount of data has been transferred. If the option value is set to\n   403→`{infinity, infinity}`, no rekeying will be initiated. Note that rekeying\n   404→initiated by the peer will still be performed.\n   405→\"\"\".\n   406→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   407→-type limit_time()  :: pos_integer() | infinity .\n   408→\n   409→-doc \"\"\"\n   410→Module implementing the behaviour `m:ssh_client_key_api` and/or\n   411→`m:ssh_server_key_api`. The\n   564→  default is to ask the user with a question on stdio of whether to accept or\n   565→  reject the new Host Key. See the option\n   566→  [`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) for specifying the path to\n   567→  the file `known_hosts` where previously accepted Host Keys are recorded. See\n   568→  also the option [key_cb](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) for the general way to\n   569→  handle keys.\n   570→\n   571→  The option can be given in three different forms as seen\n   572→  [above](`t:accept_hosts/0`):\n   573→\n   574→  - The value is a `t:boolean/0`. The value `true` will make the client accept\n   575→    any unknown Host Key without any user interaction. The value `false`\n   576→    preserves the default behaviour of asking the user on stdio.\n   577→  - An `t:accept_callback/0` will be called and the boolean return value `true`\n   578→    will make the client accept the Host Key. A return value of `false` will\n   579→    make the client to reject the Host Key and as a result the connection will\n   580→    be closed. The arguments to the fun are:\n   581→    - `PeerName` \\- a string with the name or address of the remote host.\n   582→    - `FingerPrint` \\- the fingerprint of the Host Key as\n   583→      `hostkey_fingerprint/1` calculates it.\n   584→  - A tuple `{HashAlgoSpec, accept_callback}`. The `HashAlgoSpec` specifies\n   585→    which hash algorithm shall be used to calculate the fingerprint used in the\n   586→    call of the `t:accept_callback/0`. The `HashALgoSpec` is either an atom or a\n   587→    list of atoms as the first argument in `hostkey_fingerprint/2`. If it is a\n   588→    list of hash algorithm names, the `FingerPrint` argument in the\n   589→    `t:accept_callback/0` will be a list of fingerprints in the same order as\n   590→    the corresponding name in the `HashAlgoSpec` list.\n   591→\n   592→- **`user_interaction`** - If `false`, disables the client to connect to the\n   593→  server if any user interaction is needed, such as accepting the server to be\n   594→  added to the `known_hosts` file, or supplying a password.\n   595→\n   596→  Even if user interaction is allowed it can be suppressed by other options,\n   597→  such as `silently_accept_hosts` and `password`. However, those options are not\n   598→  always desirable to use from a security point of view.\n   599→\n   600→  Defaults to `true`.\n   601→\n   602→- **`save_accepted_host`** - If `true`, the client saves an accepted host key to\n   603→  avoid the accept question the next time the same host is connected. If the\n   604→  option [`key_cb`](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) is not present, the key is saved\n   605→  in the file \"known_hosts\". See option\n   606→  [`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) for the location of that\n   607→  file.\n   608→\n   609→  If `false`, the key is not saved and the key will still be unknown at the next\n   610→  access of the same host.\n   611→\n   612→  Defaults to `true`\n   613→\n   614→- **`quiet_mode`** - If `true`, the client does not print anything on\n   615→  authorization.\n   616→\n   617→  Defaults to `false`\n   618→\"\"\".\n   619→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   620→-type host_accepting_client_options() ::\n   621→        {silently_accept_hosts, accept_hosts()}\n   622→      | {user_interaction,     boolean()}\n   623→      | {save_accepted_host,   boolean()}\n   624→      | {quiet_mode,           boolean()} .\n   625→\n   626→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   627→-type accept_hosts() :: boolean()\n   628→                      | accept_callback()\n   629→                      | {HashAlgoSpec::fp_digest_alg(), accept_callback()}.\n   630→\n   631→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   632→-type fp_digest_alg() :: 'md5' | crypto:sha1() | crypto:sha2() .\n   633→\n   634→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   635→-type accept_callback() :: fun((PeerName::string(), fingerprint() ) -> boolean()) % Old style\n   636→                         | fun((PeerName::string(), Port::inet:port_number(), fingerprint() ) -> boolean()) % New style\n   637→                           .\n   638→\n   639→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   640→-type fingerprint() :: string() | [string()].\n   641→\n   642→-doc \"\"\"\n   643→- **`user`** - Provides the username. The default is to use the Erlang shell: `{shell, start, []}`\n   740→\n   741→See the option [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) for a description of\n   742→how the daemon executes shell-requests and exec-requests depending on the shell-\n   743→and exec-options.\n   744→\"\"\".\n   745→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   746→-type 'shell_fun/2'() :: fun((User::string(),  PeerAddr::inet:ip_address()) -> pid()).\n   747→\n   748→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   749→-type exec_daemon_option()      :: {exec, exec_spec()} .\n   750→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   751→-type exec_spec()               :: {direct, exec_fun()} | disabled | deprecated_exec_opt().\n   752→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   753→-type exec_fun()                :: 'exec_fun/1'() | 'exec_fun/2'() | 'exec_fun/3'().\n   754→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   755→-type 'exec_fun/1'() :: fun((Cmd::string()) -> exec_result()) .\n   756→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   757→-type 'exec_fun/2'() :: fun((Cmd::string(), User::string()) -> exec_result()) .\n   758→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   759→-type 'exec_fun/3'() :: fun((Cmd::string(), User::string(), ClientAddr::ip_port()) -> exec_result()) .\n   760→-doc \"\"\"\n   761→This option changes how the daemon executes exec-requests from clients. The term\n   762→in the return value is formatted to a string if it is a non-string type. No\n   763→trailing newline is added in the ok-case.\n   764→\n   765→See the User's Guide section on\n   766→[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n   767→\n   768→Error texts are returned on channel-type 1 which usually is piped to `stderr` on\n   769→e.g Linux systems. Texts from a successful execution are returned on\n   770→channel-type 0 and will in similar manner be piped to `stdout`. The exit-status\n   771→code is set to 0 for success and 255 for errors. The exact results presented on\n   772→the client side depends on the client and the client's operating system.\n   773→\n   774→In case of the `{direct, exec_fun()}` variant or no exec-option at all, all\n   775→reads from `standard_input` will be from the received data-events of type 0.\n   776→Those are sent by the client. Similarly all writes to `standard_output` will be\n   777→sent as data-events to the client. An OS shell client like the command 'ssh'\n   778→will usually use stdin and stdout for the user interface.\n   779→\n   780→The option cooperates with the daemon-option\n   781→[`shell`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) in the following way:\n   782→\n   783→- **1\\. If neither the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) nor the\n   784→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present:** - The default Erlang\n   785→  evaluator is used both for exec and shell requests. The result is returned to\n   786→  the client.\n   787→\n   788→- **2\\. If the [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`)'s value is `disabled`\n   789→  (the [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) may or may not be\n   790→  present):** - No exec-requests are executed but shell-requests are not\n   791→  affected, they follow the [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value.\n   792→\n   793→- **3\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is present and the\n   794→  [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) value =/= `disabled` (the\n   795→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) may or may not be present):** -\n   796→  The [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) `fun()` is called with the same\n   797→  number of parameters as the arity of the fun, and the result is returned to\n   798→  the client. Shell-requests are not affected, they follow the\n   799→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value.\n   800→\n   801→- **4\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   802→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present with the default Erlang\n   803→  shell as the [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value:** - The\n   804→  default Erlang evaluator is used both for exec and shell requests. The result\n   805→  is returned to the client.\n   806→\n   807→- **5\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   808→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present with a value that is\n   809→  neither the default Erlang shell nor the value `disabled`:** - The\n   810→  exec-request is not evaluated and an error message is returned to the client.\n   811→  Shell-requests are executed according to the value of the\n   812→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`).\n   813→\n   814→- **6\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   815→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value is `disabled`:** - Exec\n   816→  requests are executed by the default shell, but shell-requests are not\n   817→  executed.\n   818→\n   819→If a custom CLI is installed (see the option\n   820→[`ssh_cli`](`t:ssh_cli_daemon_option/0`)) the rules above are replaced by thoose\n   821→implied by the custom CLI.\n   822→\n   823→> #### Note {: .info }\n   824→>\n   825→> The [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) has existed for a long time but\n   826→> has not previously been documented. The old definition and behaviour are\n   827→> retained but obey the rules 1-6 above if conflicting. The old and undocumented\n   828→> style should not be used in new programs.\n   829→\"\"\".\n   830→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   831→-type exec_result()  :: {ok,Result::term()} | {error,Reason::term()} .\n   832→-doc \"\"\"\n   833→Old-style exec specification that are kept for compatibility, but should not be\n   834→used in new programs\n   835→\"\"\".\n   836→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   837→-type deprecated_exec_opt() :: fun() | mod_fun_args() .\n   838→\n   839→-doc \"\"\"\n   840→Provides your own CLI implementation in a daemon.\n   841→\n   842→It is a channel callback module that implements a shell and command execution.\n   843→The shell's read-eval-print loop can be customized, using the option\n   844→[`shell`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`). Sets the text strings that the daemon\n   906→  sends to the client for presentation to the user when using\n   907→  `keyboard-interactive` authentication.\n   908→\n   909→  If the fun/3 or fun/4 is used, it is called when the actual authentication\n   910→  occurs and may therefore return dynamic data like time, remote ip etc.\n   911→\n   912→  The parameter `Echo` guides the client about need to hide the password.\n   913→\n   914→  The default value is:\n   915→  `{auth_method_kb_interactive_data, {\"SSH server\", \"Enter password for \\\"\"++User++\"\\\"\", \"password: \", false}>`\n   916→\n   917→- **`user_passwords`{: #option-user_passwords }** - Provides passwords for\n   918→  password authentication. The passwords are used when someone tries to connect\n   919→  to the server and public key user-authentication fails. The option provides a\n   920→  list of valid usernames and the corresponding passwords.\n   921→\n   922→  > #### Warning {: .warning }\n   923→  >\n   924→  > Note that this is very insecure due to the plain-text passwords; it is\n   925→  > intended for test purposes. Use the [`pwdfun`](`m:ssh#option-pwdfun`) option\n   926→  > to handle the password checking instead.\n   927→\n   928→- **`pk_check_user`{: #option-pk_check_user }** - Enables checking of the\n   929→  [client's user name](`t:authentication_client_options/0`) in the server when\n   930→  doing public key authentication. It is disabled by default.\n   931→\n   932→  The term \"user\" is used differently in OpenSSH and SSH in Erlang/OTP: see more\n   933→  in the [User's Guide](terminology.md#the-term-user).\n   934→\n   935→  If the option is enabled, and no [`pwdfun`](`m:ssh#option-pwdfun`) is present,\n   936→  the user name must present in the\n   937→  [user_passwords](`m:ssh#option-user_passwords`) for the check to succeed but\n   938→  the value of the password is not checked.\n   939→\n   940→  In case of a [`pwdfun`](`m:ssh#option-pwdfun`) checking the user, the atom\n   941→  `pubkey` is put in the password argument.\n   942→\n   943→- **`password`{: #option-password }** - Provides a global password that\n   944→  authenticates any user.\n   945→\n   946→  > #### Warning {: .warning }\n   947→  >\n   948→  > Intended to facilitate testing.\n   949→  >\n   950→  > From a security perspective this option makes the server very vulnerable.\n   951→\n   952→- **`pwdfun`{: #option-pwdfun } with `t:pwdfun_4/0`** - Provides a function for\n   953→  password validation. This could used for calling an external system or\n   954→  handling passwords stored as hash values.\n   955→\n   956→  This fun can also be used to make delays in authentication tries for example\n   957→  by calling `timer:sleep/1`.\n   958→\n   959→  To facilitate for instance counting of failed tries, the `State` variable\n   960→  could be used. This state is per connection only. The first time the pwdfun is\n   961→  called for a connection, the `State` variable has the value `undefined`.\n   962→\n   963→  The fun should return:\n   964→\n   965→  - `true` if the user and password is valid\n   966→  - `false` if the user or password is invalid\n   967→  - `disconnect` if a SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT message should be sent immediately. It\n   968→    will be followed by a close of the underlying tcp connection.\n   969→  - `{true, NewState:any()}` if the user and password is valid\n   970→  - `{false, NewState:any()}` if the user or password is invalid\n   971→\n   972→  A third usage is to block login attempts from a missbehaving peer. The `State`\n   973→  described above can be used for this. The return value `disconnect` is useful\n   974→  for this.\n   975→\n   976→  In case of the [`pk_check_user`](`m:ssh#option-pk_check_user`) is set, the\n   977→  atom `pubkey` is put in the password argument when validating a public key\n   978→  login. The pwdfun is then responsible to check that the user name is valid.\n   979→\n   980→- **`pwdfun` with `t:pwdfun_2/0`** - Provides a function for password\n   981→  validation. This function is called with user and password as strings, and\n   982→  returns:\n   983→\n   984→  - `true` if the user and password is valid\n   985→  - `false` if the user or password is invalid\n   986→\n   987→  In case of the [`pk_check_user`](`m:ssh#option-pk_check_user`) is set, the\n   988→  atom `pubkey` is put in the password argument when validating a public key\n   989→  login. The pwdfun is then responsible to check that the user name is valid.\n   990→\n   991→  This variant is kept for compatibility.\n   992→\n   993→- **`no_auth_needed`{: #option-no_auth_needed }** - If `true`, a client is\n   994→  authenticated without any need of providing any password or key.\n   995→\n   996→  This option is only intended for very special applications due to the high\n   997→  risk of accepting any connecting client.\n   998→\n   999→  The default value is `false`.\n  1000→\"\"\".\n  1001→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1002→-type pwdfun_4() :: fun((User::string(),\n  1003→                         Password::string()|pubkey,\n  1004→                         PeerAddress::ip_port(),\n  1005→                         State::any()) ->\n  1006→                               boolean() | disconnect | {boolean(),NewState::any()}\n  1007→                       ) .\n  1008→\n  1009→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1010→-type diffie_hellman_group_exchange_daemon_option() ::\n  1011→        {dh_gex_groups, [explicit_group()] | explicit_group_file() | ssh_moduli_file()}\n  1012→      | {dh_gex_limits, {Min::pos_integer(), Max::pos_integer()} } .\n  1013→\n  1014→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1015→-type explicit_group() :: {Size::pos_integer(),G::pos_integer(),P::pos_integer()} .\n  1016→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1017→-type explicit_group_file() :: {file,string()} .\n  1018→-doc \"\"\"\n  1019→- **`dh_gex_groups`** - Defines the groups the server may choose among when\n  1020→  diffie-hellman-group-exchange is negotiated. See\n  1021→  [RFC 4419](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4419) for details. The three\n  1022→  variants of this option are:\n  1023→\n  1024→  - **`{Size=integer(),G=integer(),P=integer()}`** - The groups are given\n  1025→    explicitly in this list. There may be several elements with the same `Size`.\n  1026→    In such a case, the server will choose one randomly in the negotiated Size.\n  1027→\n  1028→  - **`{file,filename()}`** - The file must have one or more three-tuples\n  1029→    `{Size=integer(),G=integer(),P=integer()}` terminated by a dot. The file is\n  1030→    read when the daemon starts.\n  1031→\n  1032→  - **`{ssh_moduli_file,filename()}`** - The file must be in\n  1033→    [ssh-keygen moduli file format](`public_key:dh_gex_group/4`). The file is\n  1034→    read when the daemon starts.\n  1035→\n  1036→  The default list is fetched from the [public_key](`public_key:dh_gex_group/4`)\n  1037→  application.\n  1038→\n  1039→- **`dh_gex_limits`** - Limits what a client can ask for in\n  1040→  diffie-hellman-group-exchange. The limits will be\n  1041→  `{MaxUsed = min(MaxClient,Max), MinUsed = max(MinClient,Min)}` where\n  1042→  `MaxClient` and `MinClient` are the values proposed by a connecting client.\n  1043→\n  1044→  The default value is `{0,infinity}`.\n  1045→\n  1046→  If `MaxUsed < MinUsed` in a key exchange, it will fail with a disconnect.\n  1047→\n  1048→  See [RFC 4419](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4419) for the function of the\n  1049→  Max and Min values.\n  1050→\"\"\".\n  1051→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1052→-type ssh_moduli_file() :: {ssh_moduli_file,string()}.\n  1053→\n  1054→-doc \"\"\"\n  1055→Maximum time in milliseconds for the first channel start after completion of the\n  1056→authentication negotiation. Defaults to `infinity`.\n  1057→\n  1058→For more information about timeouts, see the\n  1059→[Timeouts section ](hardening.md#timeouts)in the User's Guide\n  1060→[Hardening](hardening.md) chapter.\n  1061→\"\"\".\n  1062→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1063→-type max_initial_idle_time_daemon_option() :: {max_initial_idle_time, timeout()} .\n  1064→-doc \"\"\"\n  1065→Maximum time in milliseconds for the authentication negotiation. Defaults to\n  1066→120000 ms (2 minutes). If the client fails to log in within this time, the\n  1067→connection is closed.\n  1068→\n  1069→For more information about timeouts, see the\n  1070→[Timeouts section ](hardening.md#timeouts)in the User's Guide\n  1071→[Hardening](hardening.md) chapter.\n  1072→\"\"\".\n  1073→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1074→-type negotiation_timeout_daemon_option() :: {negotiation_timeout, timeout()} .\n  1075→-doc \"\"\"\n  1076→Maximum time in milliseconds for the first part of the ssh session setup, the\n  1077→hello message exchange. Defaults to 30000 ms (30 seconds). If the client fails\n  1078→to send the first message within this time, the connection is closed.\n  1079→\n  1080→For more information about timeouts, see the\n  1081→[Timeouts section ](hardening.md#timeouts)in the User's Guide\n  1082→[Hardening](hardening.md) chapter.\n  1083→\"\"\".\n  1084→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1085→-type hello_timeout_daemon_option() :: {hello_timeout, timeout()} .\n  1086→\n  1087→-doc \"\"\"\n  1088→For more information about hardening, see the [Hardening](hardening.md) section\n  1089→in the User's Guide chapter.\n  1090→\n  1091→- **`max_sessions`{: #hardening_daemon_options-max_sessions }** - The maximum\n  1092→  number of simultaneous sessions that are accepted at any time for this daemon.\n  1093→  This includes sessions that are being authorized. Thus, if set to `N`, and `N`\n  1094→  clients have connected but not started the login process, connection attempt\n  1095→  `N+1` is aborted. If `N` connections are authenticated and still logged in, no\n  1096→  more logins are accepted until one of the existing ones log out.\n  1097→\n  1098→  The counter is per listening port. Thus, if two daemons are started, one with\n  1099→  `{max_sessions,N}` and the other with `{max_sessions,M}`, in total `N+M`\n  1100→  connections are accepted for the whole `ssh` application.\n  1101→\n  1102→  Notice that if `parallel_login` is `false`, only one client at a time can be\n  1103→  in the authentication phase.\n  1104→\n  1105→  By default, this option is not set. This means that the number is not limited.\n  1106→\n  1107→- **`max_channels`{: #hardening_daemon_options-max_channels }** - The maximum\n  1108→  number of channels with active remote subsystem that are accepted for each\n  1109→  connection to this daemon\n  1110→\n  1111→  By default, this option is not set. This means that the number is not limited.\n  1112→\n  1113→- **`parallel_login`{: #hardening_daemon_options-parallel_login }** - If set to\n  1114→  false (the default value), only one login is handled at a time. If set to\n  1115→  true, an unlimited number of login attempts are allowed simultaneously.\n  1116→\n  1117→  If the `max_sessions` option is set to `N` and `parallel_login` is set to\n  1118→  `true`, the maximum number of simultaneous login attempts at any time is\n  1119→  limited to `N-K`, where `K` is the number of authenticated connections present\n  1120→  at this daemon.\n  1121→\n  1122→  > #### Warning {: .warning }\n  1123→  >\n  1124→  > Do not enable `parallel_logins` without protecting the server by other\n  1125→  > means, for example, by the `max_sessions` option or a firewall\n  1126→  > configuration. If set to `true`, there is no protection against DOS attacks.\n  1127→\n  1128→- **`minimal_remote_max_packet_size`{:\n  1129→  #hardening_daemon_options-minimal_remote_max_packet_size }** - The least\n  1130→  maximum packet size that the daemon will accept in channel open requests from\n  1131→  the client. The default value is 0.\n  1132→\"\"\".\n  1133→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1134→-type hardening_daemon_options() ::\n  1135→        {max_sessions, pos_integer()}\n  1136→      | {max_channels, pos_integer()}\n  1137→      | {parallel_login, boolean()}\n  1138→      | {minimal_remote_max_packet_size, pos_integer()}.\n  1139→\n  1140→-doc \"\"\"\n  1141→- **`connectfun`** - Provides a fun to implement your own logging when a user\n  1142→  authenticates to the server.\n  1143→\n  1144→- **`failfun`** - Provides a fun to implement your own logging when a user fails\n  1145→  to authenticate.\n  1146→\"\"\".\n  1147→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1148→-type callbacks_daemon_options() ::\n  1149→        {failfun, fun((User::string(), Peer::{inet:ip_address(), inet:port_number()}, Reason::term()) -> _)}\n  1150→      | {connectfun, fun((User::string(), Peer::{inet:ip_address(), inet:port_number()}, Method::string()) ->_)} .\n  1151→\n  1152→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1153→-type opaque_daemon_options()  ::\n  1154→        {infofun, fun()}\n  1155→      | opaque_common_options().\n  1156→\n  1157→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1158→-type ip_port() :: {inet:ip_address(), inet:port_number()} .\n  1159→\n  1160→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1161→-type mod_args() :: {Module::atom(), Args::list()} .\n  1162→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1163→-type mod_fun_args() :: {Module::atom(), Function::atom(), Args::list()} .\n  1164→\n  1165→\n  1166→%% Records\n  1167→-record(address, {address,\n  1168→                  port,\n  1169→                  profile\n  1170→                 }).\n  1171→\n  1172→-record(ssh,\n  1173→\t{\n  1174→\t  role :: role(),\n  1175→\t  peer :: undefined | \n  1176→                  {inet:hostname(),ip_port()},         %% string version of peer address \n  1177→\n  1178→          local,        %% Local sockname. CRITICAL COMPONENTS: VULNERABILITY INDICATORS\n\n### **Message Parsing & Input Validation**\n\n**ssh_message.erl decode/1 function:**\n```erlang\ndecode(<<?BYTE(?SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST), ?UINT32(Recipient),\n\t ?DEC_BIN(RequestType,__0), ?BYTE(Bool), Data/binary>>=Bytes) ->\n    try\n        #ssh_msg_channel_request{\n           recipient_channel = Recipient,\n           request_type = ?unicode_list(RequestType),\n           want_reply = erl_boolean(Bool),\n           data  = Data\n          }\n    catch _:_ ->\n            %% Faulty, RFC4254 says: \"If the request is not recognized or is not\n            %% supported for the channel, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE is returned.\"\n            %% So we provoke such a message to be sent\n            #ssh_msg_channel_request{\n               recipient_channel = Recipient,\n               request_type = faulty_msg,\n               data = Bytes\n              }\n    end;\n```\n\n**Key patterns:**\n- Binary pattern matching with guards validates structure\n- Unicode validation with error handling\n- Graceful degradation for malformed messages\n- Error codes returned instead of crashes\n\n### **Authentication & Authorization**\n\n**ssh_auth.erl:**\n- Password, public key, and keyboard-interactive methods supported\n- Server-side validation through `handle_userauth_request/3`\n- User authentication flag: `#ssh.authenticated = false|true`\n- Methods: \"publickey\", \"keyboard-interactive\", \"password\"\n\n### **Encryption & Integrity**\n\n**ssh_transport.erl:**\n- Supported ciphers:\n  - AEAD ciphers validate both authentication and encryption\n  - EtM (Encrypt-then-MAC) provides better security than MtE\n  - Sequence number tracking prevents replay attacks\n\n### **Channel Access Control**\n\n**ssh_connection.erl:**\n- Recipient channel validation in all channel messages\n- Window size adjustment prevents buffer overflow\n- Data type validation for extended data (stdout/stderr)\n- Request type validation (exec, shell, pty-req, etc.)\n\n### **Potential Security-Relevant Code Patterns**\n\n1. **Unicode Handling** (ssh_message.erl, line 50-55):\n   ```erlang\n   ucl(B) ->\n       try unicode:characters_to_list(B) of\n           L when is_list(L) -> L;\n           {error,_Matched,Rest} -> throw({error,{bad_unicode,Rest}})\n   ```\n   - Proper error handling for malformed UTF-8\n\n2. **Algorithm Negotiation** (ssh_transport.erl):\n   - Verification that required algorithms are selected\n   - Prevents downgrade attacks via strict checking\n\n3. **Sequence Number Tracking** (ssh.hrl):\n   - `send_sequence` and `recv_sequence` fields track packet ordering\n   - Prevents replay attacks\n\n4. **Connection State Machine** (ssh_connection_handler.erl):\n   - Strict state enforcement prevents out-of-order operations\n   - Renegotiation handled separately with `kex_strict_negotiated` flag\n\n---\n\n## 7. Main event dispatcher:\n  - Receives SSH messages (internal events)\n  - Dispatches to protocol handlers based on message type\n  - Manages state transitions\n  - Handles disconnects, renegotiation, debugging\n\n- **Message Processing Chain**:\n  ```\n  Encrypted data → Decrypted → ssh_message:decode/1 → SSH record\n  → handle_event/4 → Protocol handler → Connection/Channel update\n  ```\n\n### **Authentication Handler (ssh_auth.erl)**\n- **`userauth_request_msg/1`** - Server-side auth verification\n- Supports auth method negotiation and fallback\n\n### **Connection Protocol (ssh_connection.erl)**\n- **`channel_open/4`** - Opens new SSH channels\n- **`exec/4`** - Executes remote command\n- **`shell/2`** - Opens remote shell\n- **`subsystem/4`** - Requests subsystem (SFTP)\n- **`send/3,4,5`** - Sends data on channel\n- **`send_eof/2`** - Sends end-of-file\n- **`setenv/5`** - Sets environment variables\n- **`window_change/4,6`** - AEAD ciphers validate both authentication and encryption\n  - EtM (Encrypt-then-MAC) provides better security than MtE\n  - Sequence number tracking prevents replay attacks\n\n### **Channel Access Control**\n\n**ssh_connection.erl:**\n- Recipient channel validation in all channel messages\n- Window size adjustment prevents buffer overflow\n- Data type validation for extended data (stdout/stderr)\n- Request type validation (exec, shell, pty-req, etc.)\n\n### **Potential Security-Relevant Code Patterns**\n\n1. **Unicode Handling** (ssh_message.erl, line 50-55):\n   ```erlang\n   ucl(B) ->\n       try unicode:characters_to_list(B) of\n           L when is_list(L) -> L;\n           {error,_Matched,Rest} -> throw({error,{bad_unicode,Rest}})\n   ```\n   - `send_sequence` and `recv_sequence` fields track packet ordering\n   - Prevents replay attacks\n\n4. **Connection State Machine** (ssh_connection_handler.erl):\n   - Strict state enforcement prevents out-of-order operations\n   - ChanData, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n   380→\n   381→open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n   382→    case       Timeout) of\n   385→        {open, Channel} ->\n   386→\t    {ok, Channel};\n   387→\tError ->\n   388→\t    Error\n   389→    end.\n   390→\n   391→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   392→%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n   393→%% execution of the given command. \n   394→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   395→-doc \"\"\"\n   396→Is to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\n   397→executing the given command. The result is several messages according to the\n   398→following pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\n   399→request is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n   400→\n   401→- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n   402→  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n   403→\n   404→- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n   405→  to be sent.\n   406→\n   407→- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n   408→  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n   409→\n   410→- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n   411→  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n   412→  not always the case.\n   413→\n   414→- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n   415→  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n   416→  shut down.\n   417→\n   418→See the User's Guide section on\n   419→[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n   420→\"\"\".\n   421→-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n   422→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   423→      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n   424→      Command :: string(),\n   425→      Timeout :: timeout().\n   426→\n   427→exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n   428→    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n   429→\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n   430→\n   431→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   432→%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n   433→%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n   434→%% end.\n   435→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   436→-doc \"\"\"\n   437→Is to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\n   438→shell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\n   439→server end.\n   440→\n   441→Note: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\n   442→this module. This is a fault that was introduced so long ago that any change\n   443→would break a large number of existing software.\n   444→\"\"\".\n   445→-spec shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> Result when\n   446→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   447→      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n   448→      Result :: ok | success | failure | {error, timeout} .\n   449→\n   450→shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) ->\n   451→    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId,\n   452→ \t\t\t\t   \"shell\", false, <<>>, 0).\n   453→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   454→%%\n   455→%% Description: Executes a predefined subsystem.\n   456→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   457→-doc \"\"\"\n   458→Is to be called by a client-channel process for requesting to execute a\n   459→predefined subsystem on the server.\n   460→\n   461→The function [`subsystem/4`](`subsystem/4`) and subsequent calls of\n   462→[send/3,4,5](`send/3`) must be executed in the same process.\n   463→\"\"\".\n   464→-spec subsystem(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Subsystem, Timeout) -> result() when\n   465→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   466→      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n   467→      Subsystem  :: string(),\n   468→      Timeout :: timeout().\n   469→\n   470→subsystem(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, SubSystem, TimeOut) ->\n   471→     ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(),\n   472→\t\t\t\t    ChannelId, \"subsystem\", \n   473→\t\t\t\t    true, [?string(SubSystem)], TimeOut).\n   474→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   475→%% Description: Sends channel data.\n   476→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   477→-doc(#{equiv => send/5}).\n   478→-spec send(connection_ref(), channel_id(), iodata()) ->\n   479→\t\t  ok | {error, reason()}.\n   480→\n   481→send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Data) ->\n   482→    send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, infinity).\n   483→\n   484→\n   485→-doc \"\"\"\n   486→send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Type, Data)\n   487→\n   488→Depending on input arguments equivalent to one of `send/5` calls specified below.\n   489→\n   490→Equivalent to [send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, TimeOut)](`send/5`) if\n   491→called with TimeOut being integer.\n   492→\n   493→Equivalent to [send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, TimeOut);\n   505→\n   506→send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Data, infinity) ->\n   507→    send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, infinity);\n   508→\n   509→send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Type, Data) ->\n   510→    send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Type, Data, infinity).\n   511→\n   512→\n   513→-doc \"\"\"\n   514→Is to be called by client- and 526→-doc \"Sends EOF on channel `ChannelId`.\".\n   527→-spec send_eof(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> ok  | {error, closed} when\n   528→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   529→ 538→-doc \"\"\"\n   539→Adjusts the SSH flow control window. Is to\n   646→be called by an SSH client process.\n   647→\n   648→Options:\n   649→\n   650→- **\\{term, string()\\}** - Defaults to _os:getenv(\"TERM\")_ or _vt100_ if it is\n   651→  undefined.\n   652→\n   653→- **\\{width, integer()\\}** - Defaults to 80 if `pixel_width` is not defined.\n   654→\n   655→- **\\{height, integer()\\}** - Defaults to 24 if `pixel_height` is not defined.\n   656→\n   657→- **\\{pixel_width, integer()\\}** - Is disregarded if `width` is defined.\n   658→\n   659→- **\\{pixel_height, integer()\\}** - Is disregarded if `height` is defined.\n   660→\n   661→- **\\{pty_opts, \\[\\{posix_atom(), integer()\\}]\\}** - Option can be an empty\n   662→  list. 1529→\tfalse ->\n  1530→\t    <<Msg1:PacketSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n  1531→\t    {WindowSize - PacketSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}}\n  1532→    end;\n The API functions in this module send SSH Connection Protocol\nevents, which are received as messages by the remote channel handling the remote\nchannel. The Erlang format of thoose messages is (see also\n[below](`t:event/0`)):\n\n`{ssh_cm,` `t:ssh:connection_ref/0` `,` `t:channel_msg/0` `}`\n\nIf the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior is used to implement the channel process,\nthese messages are handled by\n[handle_ssh_msg/2](`c:ssh_client_channel:handle_ssh_msg/2`).\n\"\"\".\n-moduledoc(#{titles =>\n                 [{type,<<\"General\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Data Transfer\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Closing a Channel\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Environment Variable\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Shell or Command\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Window Change\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Signals\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Exit Status\">>}]}).\n\n-include(\"ssh.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_connect.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_transport.hrl\").\n\n%% API\n-export([session_channel/2, session_channel/4,\n\t exec/4, connection_ref() :: ssh:connection_ref().\n-type channel_id()     :: ssh:channel_id().\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe status of a request. Corresponds to the `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS` and\n`SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE` values in\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.4](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section-5.4).\n\"\"\".\n-type req_status() :: success | failure .\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe result of a call.\n\nIf the request reached the peer, was handled and the response reached the\nrequesting node the `t:req_status/0` is the status reported from the peer.\n\nIf not, the `t:reason/0` indicates what went wrong:\n\n- **`closed`** - indicates that the channel or connection was closed when trying\n  to send the request\n\n- **`timeout`** - indicates that the operation exceeded a time limit\n\"\"\".\n-type reason() :: closed | timeout .\n\n-doc(#{}).\n-type result() :: req_status() | {error, reason()} .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe valid values are `0` (\"normal\") and `1` (\"stderr\"), see\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.2](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#page-8).\n\"\"\".\n-type ssh_data_type_code() :: non_neg_integer(). % Only 0 and 1 are used\n\n\n%%% The SSH Connection Protocol\n\n-export_type([event/0,\n              channel_msg/0,\n              want_reply/0,\n              data_ch_msg/0,\n              eof_ch_msg/0,\n              signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_status_ch_msg/0,\n              closed_ch_msg/0,\n              env_ch_msg/0,\n              pty_ch_msg/0,\n              shell_ch_msg/0,\n              window_change_ch_msg/0,\n              exec_ch_msg/0\n             ]).\n\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type event() :: {ssh_cm, ssh:connection_ref(), channel_msg()}.\n-doc \"\"\"\nAs mentioned in the introduction, the\n[SSH Connection Protocol](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254) events are\nhandled as messages. When writing a channel handling process without using the\nsupport by the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior the process must handle thoose\nmessages.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type channel_msg() ::  data_ch_msg()\n                      | eof_ch_msg()\n                      | closed_ch_msg()\n                      | pty_ch_msg()\n                      | env_ch_msg()\n                      | shell_ch_msg()\n                      | exec_ch_msg()\n                      | signal_ch_msg()\n                      | window_change_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_status_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_signal_ch_msg()\n                        .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nMessages that include a `WantReply` expect the channel handling process to call\n[ssh_connection:reply_request/4](`reply_request/4`) with the boolean value of\n`WantReply` as the second argument.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type want_reply() :: boolean().\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nData has arrived on the channel. This event is sent as a result of calling\n[ssh_connection:send/3,4,5](`send/3`).\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Data Transfer\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 5.2\"}).\n-type data_ch_msg() :: {data,\n                        ssh:channel_id(),\n                        ssh_data_type_code(),\n                        Data :: binary()\n                       } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nIndicates that the other side sends no more data. This event is sent as a result\nof calling [ssh_connection:send_eof/2](`send_eof/2`).\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Closing a Channel\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 5.3\"}).\n-type eof_ch_msg() :: {eof,\n                       ssh:channel_id()\n                      } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nA signal can be delivered to the remote process/service using the following\nmessage. Some systems do not support signals, in which case they are to ignore\nthis message. There is currently no function to generate this event as the\nsignals referred to are on OS-level and not something generated by an Erlang\nprogram.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Signals\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.9\"}).\n-type signal_ch_msg() :: {signal,\n                          ssh:channel_id(),\n                          SignalName :: string()\n                         } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nA remote execution can terminate violently because of a signal. Then this\nmessage can be received. A zero `exit_status` usually\nmeans that the command terminated successfully. This event is sent as a result\nof calling [ssh_connection:exit_status/3](`exit_status/3`).\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Exit Status\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.10\"}).\n-type exit_status_ch_msg() :: {exit_status,\n                               ssh:channel_id(),\n                               ExitStatus :: non_neg_integer()\n                              } .\n-doc             } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nEnvironment variables can be passed to the shell/command to be started later.\nThis event is sent as a result of calling <<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.2\"}).\n-type pty_ch_msg() :: {pty,\n                       ssh:channel_id(),\n                       want_reply(),\n                      PixelHeight :: non_neg_integer(),\n                        TerminalModes :: [term_mode()]\n                       }\n                      } .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nA pseudo-terminal has been requested for the session. `Terminal` is the value of\nthe TERM environment variable value, that is, `vt100`. Zero dimension parameters\nmust be ignored. The character/row dimensions override the pixel dimensions\n(when non-zero). Pixel dimensions refer to the drawable area of the window.\n`Opcode` in the `TerminalModes` list is the mnemonic name, represented as a\nlowercase Erlang atom, defined in\n[RFC 4254](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section/8), Section 8. It can\nalso be an `Opcode` if the mnemonic name is not listed in the RFC. Example:\n`OP code: 53, mnemonic name ECHO erlang atom: echo`. This event is sent as a\nresult of calling [ssh_connection:ptty_alloc/4](`ptty_alloc/4`).\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.2\"}).\n-type term_mode() :: {Opcode :: atom() | byte(),\n                      Value :: non_neg_integer()} .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThis message requests that the user default shell is started at the other end.\nThis event is sent as a result of calling [ssh_connection:shell/2](`shell/2`).\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Shell or Command\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.5\"}).\n-type shell_ch_msg() :: {shell,\n                         ssh:channel_id(),\n                         want_reply()\n                        } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nWhen the window (terminal) size changes on the client side, it _can_ send a\nmessage to the server side to inform it of the new dimensions. A session is a\n%% remote execution of a program. The program may be a shell, an\n%% application, a system command, or some built-in subsystem.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n-doc(#{equiv => session_channel/4}).\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) ->\n    session_channel(ConnectionRef, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nOpens a channel for an SSH session. The channel id returned from this function\nis the id used as input to the other functions in this module.\n\"\"\".\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      InitialWindowSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      MaxPacketSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, \"session\", <<>>,\n                 InitialWindowSize,\n                 MaxPacketSize,\n                 Timeout).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Opens a channel for the given type.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc false.\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    case ssh_connection_handler:open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData,\n                                             InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize,\n                                             Timeout) of\n        {open, Channel} ->\n\t    {ok, Channel};\n\tError ->\n\t    Error\n    end.\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n%% execution of the given command. \n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\nexecuting the given command. The result is several messages according to the\nfollowing pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\nrequest is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n\n- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n  to be sent.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n  not always the case.\n\n- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n  shut down.\n\nSee the User's Guide section on\n[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n\"\"\".\n-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Command :: string(),\n      Timeout :: timeout().\n\nexec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n%% end.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\nshell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\nserver end.\n\nNote: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\nthis module. This is a fault that was introduced so long ago that any change\nwould break a large number of existing software.\n\"\"\".\n-spec shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Result :: ok | success | failure | {error, timeout} .\n\nshell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) ->\n    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId,\n \t\t\t\t   \"shell\", false, <<>>, 0).\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%%\n%% Description: Executes a predefined subsystem.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client-channel process for requesting to execute a\npredefined subsystem on the server.\n\nThe function [`subsystem/4`](`subsystem/4`) and subsequent calls of\n[send/3,4,5](`send/3`) must be executed in the same process.\n\"\"\".\n-spec subsystem(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Subsystem, Timeout) -> result() when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Subsystem  :: string(),\n      Timeout :: timeout().\n\nsubsystem(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, SubSystem, TimeOut) ->\n     ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(),\n\t\t\t\t    ChannelId, \"subsystem\", \n\t\t\t\t    true, [?string(SubSystem)], TimeOut).\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Sends channel Data},\n\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(SsName,_SsLen)>> = Data,\n    #channel{remote_id=RemoteId} = Channel = \n\tssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId), \n    Reply =\n        case start_subsystem(SsName, Connection, Channel,\n                             {subsystem, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(SsName)}) of\n            {ok, Pid} ->\n                erlang:monitor(process, Pid),\n                ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel#channel{user=Pid}),\n                channel_success_msg(RemoteId);\n            {error,_Error} ->\n                channel_failure_msg(RemoteId)\n        end,\n    {[{connection_reply,Reply}], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"subsystem\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore subsystem requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"pty-req\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(BTermName,_TermLen),\n      ?UINT32(Width),?UINT32(Height),\n      ?UINT32(PixWidth), ?UINT32(PixHeight),\n      Modes/binary>> = Data,\n    TermName = binary_to_list(BTermName),\n    PtyOpts0 = decode_pty_opts(Modes),\n    PtyOpts = case proplists:get_value(onlcr, PtyOpts0, undefined) of\n                  undefined ->\n                      %% If - peer client asked for pty\n                      %%    - did not tell if LF->CRLF expansion is wanted\n                      %% then\n                      %%    - do LF->CRLF expansion\n                      [{onlcr,1} | PtyOpts0];\n                  _ ->\n                      PtyOpts0\n              end,\n    PtyRequest = {TermName, Width, Height,\n\t\t  PixWidth, PixHeight, PtyOpts},\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {pty, ChannelId, WantReply, PtyRequest});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"pty-req\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore pty requests. See RFC 4254 6.2.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"shell\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {shell, ChannelId, WantReply});\n \nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"shell\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore shell requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Command, _Len)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(Command)});\n\t\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"exec\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore exec requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n  \t\t\t\t    request_type = \"env\",\n  \t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n  \t\t\t\t    data = Data}, \n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Var,_VarLen), ?DEC_BIN(Value,_ValLen)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n \t\t   {env, ChannelId, WantReply, Var, Value});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"env\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore env requests. \n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n                                    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    %% Not a valid request_type. All valid types are handling the\n    %% parameter checking in their own clauses above.\n    %% \n    %% The special ReqType faulty_msg signals that something went\n    %% wrong found during decoding.\n    %%\n    %% RFC4254 5.4 says:\n    %% \"If 'want reply' is FALSE, no response will be sent to the request.\n    %%  Otherwise, the recipient responds with either\n    %%  SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE, or request-specific\n    %%  continuation messages.  If the request is not recognized or is not\n    %%  supported for the channel, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE is returned.\"\n    %%\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId) of\n        #channel{remote_id = RemoteId} when WantReply==true -> \n            FailMsg = channel_failure_msg(RemoteId),\n            {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n        _ -> %% Channel has been closed or no reply is wanted\n            {[], Connection}\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = <<\"tcpip-forward\">>,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = <<?DEC_BIN(ListenAddrStr,_Len),?UINT32(ListenPort)>>},\n           #connection{options = Opts} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(tcpip_tunnel_out, Opts) of\n        false ->\n            %% This daemon instance has not enabled tcpip_forwarding\n            {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n        true ->\n            ConnectionSup = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts),\n            FwdSup = ssh_connection_sup:tcpip_fwd_supervisor(ConnectionSup),\n            ConnPid = self(),\n            case ssh_tcpip_forward_acceptor:supervised_start(FwdSup,\n                                                             {ListenAddrStr, ListenPort},\n                                                             undefined,\n                                                             \"forwarded-tcpip\", ssh_tcpip_forward_srv,\n                                                             ConnPid) of\n                {ok,ListenPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {ok,LPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<?UINT32(LPort)>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {error,_} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ ->\n                    {[], Connection}\n            end\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = _Type,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = _Data}, Connection, _Role, _SSH) ->\n    if WantReply == true ->\n\t    FailMsg = request_failure_msg(),\n\t    {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n       true ->\n\t    {[], Connection}  \n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {failure, <<>>}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From,_} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {failure, <<>>}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From, Fun} | Rest]} = Connection0, _, _SSH) ->\n    Connection = Fun({success,Data}, Connection0),\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_disconnect{code = Code,\n\t\t\t       description = Description},\n\t   Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {disconnect, {Code, Description}, handle_stop(Connection)}.\n\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Returns pending responses to be delivered to the peer when a\n%%% Channel/Connection closes\n%%%\n-doc false.\nhandle_stop(#connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection0) ->\n    {Connection, Replies} = \n\tssh_client_channel:cache_foldl(\n          fun(Channel, {Connection1, Acc}) ->\n                  {Reply, Connection2} =\n                      reply_msg(Channel, Connection1,\n                                {closed, Channel#channel.local_id}),\n                  {Connection2, Reply ++ Acc}\n          end, {Connection0, []}, Cache),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_delete(Cache),\n    {Replies, Connection}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% channel_*_msg(...)\n%%% Returns a #ssh_msg_....{} for channel operations.\n%%%\n-doc false.\nchannel_adjust_window_msg(ChannelId, Bytes) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_window_adjust{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t   bytes_to_add = Bytes}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_close_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_close {recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, 0, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t  data = Data};\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, Type, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_extended_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    data_type_code = Type,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_eof_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_eof{recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_failure_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_failure{recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_msg(Type, ChannelId, WindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open{channel_type = Type,\n\t\t\t  sender_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t  initial_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t  maximum_packet_size = MaxPacketSize,\n\t\t\t  data = Data\n\t\t\t }.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_confirmation_msg(RemoteId, LID, WindowSize, PacketSize) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open_confirmation{recipient_channel = RemoteId,\n\t\t\t\t       sender_channel = LID,\n\t\t\t\t       initial_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t       maximum_packet_size = PacketSize}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_failure_msg(RemoteId, Reason, Description, Lang) ->\n #ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%%\n-doc false.\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_tuple(Addr) ->\n    case catch inet_parse:ntoa(Addr) of\n\t{'EXIT',_} -> false;\n\tA -> A\n    end;\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_list(Addr) ->\n    case inet_parse:address(Addr) of\n\t{ok, _} -> Addr;\n\tError ->\n\t    case inet:getaddr(Addr, inet) of\n\t\t{ok, A} ->\n\t\t    inet_parse:ntoa(A);\n\t\tError -> false\n\t    end\n    end.\n\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n%%%\n%%% Internal functions\n%%%\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Create the channel data when an ssh_msg_open_channel message\n%%% of \"session\" typ is handled\n%%%\nsetup_session(#connection{channel_cache = Cache,\n                          channel_id_seed = NewChannelID,\n                          suggest_window_size = WinSz,\n                          suggest_packet_size = PktSz\n\t\t\t } = C,\n\t      RemoteId, Type, WindowSize, PacketSize) when is_integer(WinSz),\n                                                           is_integer(PktSz) ->\n    NextChannelID = NewChannelID + 1,\n    Channel =\n        #channel{type = Type,\n                 sys = \"ssh\",\n                 local_id NextChannelID}}.\n\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Start a cli or subsystem\n%%%\nstart_cli(#connection{options = Options, \n\t\t      cli_spec = CliSpec,\n\t\t      exec = Exec,\n\t\t      connection_supervisor = ConnectionSup}, ChannelId) ->\n    case CliSpec of\n        no_cli ->\n            {error, cli_disabled};\n        {CbModule, Args} ->\n            ssh_connection_sup:start_channel(server, ConnectionSup, self(), CbModule, ChannelId, Args, Exec, Options)\n    end.\n\n\nstart_subsystem(BinName, #connection{options = Options,\n                                     connection_supervisor = ConnectionSup},\n\t       #channel{local_id = ChannelId}, _ReplyMsg) ->\n    Name = binary_to_list(BinName),\n    case check_subsystem(Name, Options) of\n\t{Callback, Opts} when is_atom(Callback), Callback =/= none ->\n            ssh_connection_sup:start_channel(server, ConnectionSup, self(), Callback, ChannelId, Opts, undefined, Options);\n        {none, _} ->\n            {error, bad_subsystem};\n\t{_, _} ->\n\t    {error, legacy_option_not_supported}\n    end.\n\n\n%%% Helpers for starting cli/subsystems\ncheck_subsystem(\"sftp\"= SsName, Options) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(subsystems, Options) of\n\tno_subsys -> \t% FIXME: Can 'no_subsys' ever be matched?\n\t    {SsName, {Cb, Opts}} = ssh_sftpd:subsystem_spec([]),\n\t    {Cb, Opts};\n\tSubSystems ->\n\t    proplists:get_value(SsName, SubSystems, {none, []})\n    end;\n\ncheck_subsystem(SsName, Options) ->\n    Subsystems = ?GET_OPT(subsystems, Options),\n    case proplists:get_value(SsName, Subsystems, {none, []}) of\n\tFun when is_function(Fun) ->\n\t    {Fun, []};\n\t{_, _} = Value ->\n\t    Value\n    end.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Send-window handling\n%%%\n\nupdate_send_window(Channel, _, undefined,\n\t\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache}) ->\n    do_update_send_window(Channel, Cache);\n\nupdate_send_window(#channel{send_buf = SendBuffer} = Channel, DataType, Data,\n\t\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache}) ->\n    do_update_send_window(Channel#channel{send_buf = queue:in({DataType, Data}, SendBuffer)},\n\t\t\t  Cache).\n\ndo_update_send_window(Channel0, Cache) ->\n    {SendMsgs, Channel} = get_window(Channel0, []),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel), \n    {SendMsgs, Channel}.\n\nget_window(#channel{send_window_size = 0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc) ->\n    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel};\nget_window(#channel{send_packet_size = 0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc) ->\n    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel};\nget_window(#channel{send_buf = Buffer, \n\t\t    send_packet_size = PacketSize,\n\t\t    send_window_size = WindowSize0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc0) ->\n    case queue:out(Buffer) of\n\t{{value, {_, Data} = Msg}, NewBuffer} ->\n\t    case handle_send_window(Msg, byte_size(Data), PacketSize, WindowSize0, Acc0) of\n\t\t{WindowSize, Acc, {_, <<>>}} ->\n\t\t    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel#channel{send_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t send_buf = NewBuffer}};\n\t\t{WindowSize, Acc, Rest} ->\n\t\t    get_window(Channel#channel{send_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t\t       send_buf = queue:in_r(Rest, NewBuffer)}, Acc)\n\t    end;\n\t{empty, NewBuffer} ->\n\t    {[], Channel#channel{send_buf = NewBuffer}}\n    end.\n\nhandle_send_window(Msg = {Type, Data}, Size, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) when Size =< WindowSize ->\n    case Size =< PacketSize of\n\ttrue ->\n\t    {WindowSize - Size, [Msg | Acc], {Type, <<>>}};\n\tfalse ->\n\t    <<Msg1:PacketSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n\t    {WindowSize - PacketSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}}\n    end;\nhandle_send_window({Type, Data}, _, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) when WindowSize =< PacketSize ->\n    <<Msg1:WindowSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n    {WindowSize - WindowSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}};\nhandle_send_window({Type, Data}, _, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) ->\n    <<Msg1:PacketSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n    {WindowSize - PacketSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Flow control\n%%% \n\nflow_control(Channel, Cache) ->\n    flow_control([window_adjusted], Channel, Cache).\n\nflow_control([], Channel, Cache) ->\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel),\n    [];\nflow_control([_|_], #channel{flow_control = [].\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Pseudo terminal stuff\n%%% \n\npty_req(ConnectionRef, Channel, Term, Width, Height,\n\t PixWidth, PixHeight, <<?STRING(Bin)>>.\n\nencode_pty_opts2([]) -> \n    [?TTY_OP_END];\nencode_pty_opts2([{vintr,Value} | Opts]) ->\n    [?VINTR, ?uint32(Value) | encode_pty_opts2(Opts)];\nencode_pty_opts2([{vquit,Value}
